# Skills · K · part 22

832 entries, koha-dashboard-queue to kontrolle-abwaegungsentscheidungen. [Index](README.md) · [Everything](../README.md)

- **koha-dashboard-queue**: Read a Koha dashboard queue (Ready to push, Needs QA, Needs signoff, Needs triage, Needs release notes) or a user's section (Failed QA, Patch doesn't apply) from dashboard.koha-community.org via its read-only JSON API, using a personal Bugzilla API key in KOHA_DASHBOARD_API_KEY. Use for "what's in Ready to push", "show my Failed QA", "list the queue", then hand bugs to other skills. Not for applying, reviewing or fetching one bug (koha-bz-apply / koha-review / koha-bz-fetch). Args (optional): needs-push | user &lt;id&gt; &lt;group&gt;/&lt;section&gt;. ([mrenvoize/koha-contributor](https://github.com/mrenvoize/koha-contributor/tree/HEAD/skills/koha-dashboard-queue) · ★2)
- **koha-prove**: Run Koha Perl tests inside the KTD container. Pass a test file or directory path (relative to the Koha repo root, or absolute). Handles both t/ and t/db_dependent/ automatically. ([mrenvoize/koha-contributor](https://github.com/mrenvoize/koha-contributor/tree/HEAD/skills/koha-prove) · ★2)
- **koha-qa**: Run the Koha QA script (koha-qa.pl) inside KTD before submitting patches. Surfaces critic, POD, file_permissions, tinyint_has_boolean_flag, and other QA failures the community reviewers will check. Run after every commit; fix flagged issues before pushing. ([mrenvoize/koha-contributor](https://github.com/mrenvoize/koha-contributor/tree/HEAD/skills/koha-qa) · ★2)
- **koha-review**: QA review a Koha patchset for regressions, security, code quality, and Koha-specific conventions. Use when the user says "QA this", "review this patchset/branch/PR", "review my work", or "review N commits". Auto-detects patchset scope, runs specialist review agents in parallel based on file types touched, compares behaviour across preference/configuration combinations before and after the patch when it changes settings-dependent behaviour, and synthesises findings into Critical/Important/Suggestion buckets with file:line citations. ([mrenvoize/koha-contributor](https://github.com/mrenvoize/koha-contributor/tree/HEAD/skills/koha-review) · ★2)
- **koha-review-walkthrough**: Record side-by-side before/after Playwright walkthrough videos of a Koha UI patchset across different preference and configuration combinations, plus a compare player and a results table. Use when reviewing or QAing a patch that changes a UI flow or behaviour governed by system preferences or circulation rules, when the user asks for walkthrough videos, a before-and-after comparison, or a browser-test prompt for a bug, or from koha-review Step 7. Arguments (optional): bug number and the settings or scenarios to cover. ([mrenvoize/koha-contributor](https://github.com/mrenvoize/koha-contributor/tree/HEAD/skills/koha-review-walkthrough) · ★2)
- **koha-rm-queue**: Run the Koha release-manager loop over the dashboard's "Ready to push" queue: apply, QA, RM follow-ups, DBRev, sign-off trailers, then (maintainer signs) verify, push main:main and update Bugzilla. Use when the maintainer says "work the Ready to push queue", "let's go through the RM queue", "RM queue" or "push the queue". The maintainer gates signing, pushing and every Bugzilla write. Not for single-bug patch work (koha-bz-apply / koha-review / koha-qa), reading a queue (koha-dashboard-queue), or generic git push questions. ([mrenvoize/koha-contributor](https://github.com/mrenvoize/koha-contributor/tree/HEAD/skills/koha-rm-queue) · ★2)
- **koha-schema-apply**: Apply a Koha DB schema change end-to-end after editing kohastructure.sql and the atomicupdate file. Runs updatedatabase.pl in KTD, regenerates DBIC schema with dbic --force, and reminds about the manual is_boolean / relationship edits below the marker line. Use this after the atomicupdate skill has scaffolded the file and the SQL is filled in. ([mrenvoize/koha-contributor](https://github.com/mrenvoize/koha-contributor/tree/HEAD/skills/koha-schema-apply) · ★2)
- **koha-security-verify**: Prove a Koha security fix works, in a disposable KTD instance: a fail-then-pass regression test, and a browser or curl test plan with setup, reproduce before the fix, repeat after, and a control. Covers the traps that give false confirmations (Basic Auth ignored on CGI scripts, stale Plack code, timing noise in blind SQLi, a test that passes on both reverted and patched code). Use when fixing or QAing a patch for a restricted Koha security bug, XSS, SQL injection, IDOR, auth or 2FA bypass, SSRF, or when security QA says the test plan isn't sufficient. Arguments (optional): bug number. ([mrenvoize/koha-contributor](https://github.com/mrenvoize/koha-contributor/tree/HEAD/skills/koha-security-verify) · ★2)
- **koha-syspref**: Add a new Koha system preference. Generates the atomicupdate INSERT, adds the row to installer/data/mysql/mandatory/sysprefs.sql for fresh installs, and edits the matching admin/preferences/*.pref YAML so the syspref appears in the staff UI. Arguments: bug number, preference name, and a hint about which preferences page (e.g. patrons, circulation, opac). ([mrenvoize/koha-contributor](https://github.com/mrenvoize/koha-contributor/tree/HEAD/skills/koha-syspref) · ★2)
- **koha-workflow**: Choose and follow a Koha development workflow - either a single shared KTD instance (`kohadev`) with branches switched in one clone, or one git worktree plus one named, proxied KTD instance per bug (`bug_NNNNN`). - **kolmogorov-smirnov-cumulative-shift-testing**: Use when you have a DESeq2 results table plus gene sets (e.g., mer7m8/mer8 seed-match targets vs. background) and need to test for cumulative down-regulation attributed to siRNA seed region activity. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/kolmogorov-smirnov-cumulative-shift-testing))
- **kolmogorov-smirnov-distribution-comparison**: Use when two sets of network modules each carry per-gene significance/score values and the question is whether one method's modules are enriched for high-scoring genes relative to the other (KS p-value comparison). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/kolmogorov-smirnov-distribution-comparison))
- **kolmogorov-smirnov-distribution-comparison**: Use when benchmarking a synthetic-data simulator against real single-cell data by testing whether distributions of per-peak and per-cell summary statistics (mean, variance, CV, zero proportion, library size) deviate from the real matrix. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/kolmogorov-smirnov-distribution-comparison))
- **kolmogorov-smirnov-enrichment-testing**: Use when you have DESeq2 differential expression results annotated with seed-match counts (e.g. mer7m8 or mer8 matches, or a curated miRNA-target list) and want to test whether the target gene set shows a cumulative shift in log2FC relative to background. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/kolmogorov-smirnov-enrichment-testing))
- **kolmogorov-smirnov-goodness-of-fit-testing**: Use when validating that null P-value distributions from a statistical association method are uniform (unbiased), e.g. stratifying gene pairs into expression-level bins and testing each bin's P-value histogram against Uniform[0,1]. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/kolmogorov-smirnov-goodness-of-fit-testing))
- **kolmogorov-smirnov-null-uniformity-testing**: Use when validating that a DE method (e.g. Normalisr linear models) produces uniformly distributed null P values across gene expression levels, using replicate null groupings such as gRNA-free cells split randomly. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/kolmogorov-smirnov-null-uniformity-testing))
- **kolmogorov-smirnov-pancancer-enrichment-aggregation**: Use when replicating the pan-cancer iEDGE evaluation: per-type Rank-1 cis driver lists are available and you must test known-driver enrichment per cancer type and then assess aggregate significance (15/19 types FDR&lt;0.05, KS p=2.5e-13). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/kolmogorov-smirnov-pancancer-enrichment-aggregation))
- **kolmogorov-smirnov-pseudotime-distribution-comparison**: Use when a common trajectory (e.g. slingshot lineages with slingPseudotime) has been inferred across multiple experimental conditions and you need to test whether pseudotime distributions differ between conditions per lineage or globally. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/kolmogorov-smirnov-pseudotime-distribution-comparison))
- **kolmogorov-smirnov-two-sample-testing**: Use when comparing raw nanopore signal level distributions (native vs WGA control) at reference k-mer positions to flag k-mers whose signal shifts suggest a modified base. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/kolmogorov-smirnov-two-sample-testing))
- **kolmogorov-smirnov-two-sample-testing**: Use when comparing scHi-C contact counts per bin pair between exactly two cell groups (e.g. oocyte vs zygote), as a non-negative count.table matrix with a group factor, and no distributional assumption on counts is desired. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/kolmogorov-smirnov-two-sample-testing))
- **kolmogorovarnold-networks-for-metal-surface-defect-classification**: **arXiv ID:** 2501.06389 **Authors:** Maciej Krzywda, Mariusz Wermiński, Szymon Łukasik, Amir H. Gandomi **Published:** 2025-01-10T23:58:30Z **Abstract:** This paper presents the application of Kolmogorov-Arnold Networks (KAN) in classifying metal surface defects. - **konsylium**: Użyj przy nietrywialnej lub trudnej do odwrócenia decyzji (architektura, kontrakt/interfejs, bezpieczeństwo, koszt, migracja), przy wyborze z 2–3 alternatyw, stress-teście opcji do której się skłaniasz, przy pisaniu design doc/ADR, albo gdy model lub użytkownik krąży w kółko (2+ nieudane próby, powtarzane propozycje). NIE używaj do pytań trywialnych/faktograficznych, czystej egzekucji („po prostu zrób") ani jako bramki merge — konsylium to PRE-CHECK dywergencyjny, decyduje człowiek. Dla bramki wymagającej niezależności rodzin modeli → Tryb B (routing cross-model). ([witczakm/konsylium](https://github.com/witczakm/konsylium/tree/HEAD/skills/konsylium-pl) · ★1)
- **konsylium**: Use for a non-trivial or hard-to-reverse decision (architecture, contract/interface, security, cost, migration), when choosing between 2–3 alternatives, when stress-testing an option you already lean toward, when writing a design doc/ADR, or when the model or the user keeps circling (2+ failed attempts, repeated proposals). Do NOT use for trivial/factual questions, pure execution ("just do it"), or as a merge gate — the council is a divergence PRE-CHECK and a human decides. For a gate that needs independent model families → Mode B (cross-model routing). ([witczakm/konsylium](https://github.com/witczakm/konsylium/tree/HEAD/skills/konsylium) · ★1)
